### P0 - Must Fix Before Real Users

#### Public Site Access Leaks Private Gate Data

Problem

- The public site frontend appears to select or hydrate sensitive fields such as `site_password_hash`, `guest_access_token`, `privacy_mode`, and `hide_from_search`. Password hashes and invite tokens must never be sent to the browser.

Risk

- Private site gate controls can be bypassed or reverse engineered from browser-visible data.
- Password hashes, access tokens, or privacy flags reaching the client materially increase account, guest, and privacy exposure risk.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Public site data loaders and public site query helpers
- Site page routes and public hydration contracts
- Shared site selection helpers, DTO shaping, and Supabase select strings
- Any public builder preview/readback path that reuses full dashboard models

Acceptance criteria

- Public site payloads never include `site_password_hash`, `guest_access_token`, or equivalent secret-bearing/private gate fields.
- Public site payloads include only the minimum safe public fields required for rendering.
- Automated regression proof fails if sensitive columns are selected for public flows.

Suggested implementation approach

- Introduce an explicit public-site data contract with allowlisted fields only.
- Split dashboard/private site reads from public site reads instead of reusing broad record selectors.
- Add regression tests for public select strings and live/public readback proof.

#### Public Site Privacy/Password/Invite Gating Must Move Server-Side

Problem

- Privacy enforcement appears to rely too heavily on frontend knowledge of `privacy_mode`, invite state, or password-gated site data. Validation should happen server-side through an edge function or RPC that returns only safe public data after access is cleared.

Risk

- Frontend-driven gating can be bypassed, cached incorrectly, or leak enough state to help unauthorized access attempts.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Public site gate and site access flows
- Supabase edge functions/RPC candidates for public site resolution
- Password/invite validation handlers
- Guest-facing public route loaders and gate UI state

Acceptance criteria

- Public/private/password/invite gate decisions are enforced server-side.
- Unauthorized callers receive only minimal safe denial state, not private site payloads.
- Public render flows consume a safe server response contract rather than private raw site rows.

Suggested implementation approach

- Create a dedicated public-site access resolver in an edge function or RPC.
- Validate password/invite/visibility rules there, then return sanitized public site data only.
- Refactor frontend gate UI to consume the resolved access result instead of direct private fields.

#### RSVP Lookup Is Too Permissive

Problem

- The RSVP validation edge function appears to use service-role access and may allow guest lookup by name/token in ways that expose guest email, invite token, or other sensitive fields.

Risk

- Guest enumeration, privacy leakage, and unintended household data exposure become possible through RSVP lookups.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- RSVP validation/search edge functions
- RSVP page loaders and submit handlers
- Guest/household selection helpers and Supabase queries
- Proof scripts covering guest RSVP operations

Acceptance criteria

- RSVP lookup returns only the minimum fields required to identify the guest session safely.
- Guest email, raw invite tokens, and unrelated household/private fields are not exposed to the browser.
- Enumeration protections and rate limits are in place for lookup attempts.

Suggested implementation approach

- Narrow RSVP lookup queries to a safe DTO.
- Replace broad service-role reads with scoped server validation logic and minimal response objects.
- Add regression tests for enumeration/leakage and targeted rate-limit coverage.

#### RSVP Flow Should Not Return Invite Tokens To The Browser

Problem

- The current RSVP flow appears to return invite tokens to the browser. That should be replaced with a short-lived server-generated RSVP session or scoped access token.

Risk

- Long-lived invite tokens in the browser can be replayed, copied, or leaked through logs, storage, browser history, or support tooling.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- RSVP edge functions and browser session setup
- Guest invite-token generation/validation helpers
- Guest-facing RSVP routes and local/session storage
- Any email/deep-link flow that hands tokens to frontend code

Acceptance criteria

- Browser flows do not receive raw durable invite tokens after lookup/validation.
- RSVP state is carried by a short-lived scoped session/access artifact with revocation/expiry semantics.
- Existing guest RSVP update flow still works across refresh/navigation within the intended session window.

Suggested implementation approach

- Keep raw invite tokens server-side and exchange them for a short-lived RSVP session.
- Scope the returned artifact to the specific guest/household and action set.
- Remove any browser persistence of durable guest access tokens.

#### OpenAI API Key Exposure

Problem

- Any frontend use of `VITE_OPENAI_API_KEY` or direct browser-side OpenAI calls must be removed. OpenAI calls belong in Supabase edge functions or backend routes only.

Risk

- Exposed model keys can be stolen, abused, rotated out from under production, or used to access sensitive prompts/data indirectly.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Vite env usage across `src/`
- AI hooks/components/services invoked from the browser
- Edge functions and backend routes used for AI orchestration
- Docs/env examples that may encourage unsafe frontend setup

Acceptance criteria

- No frontend bundle references `VITE_OPENAI_API_KEY` or direct OpenAI browser calls.
- All model-backed calls run through approved server routes/functions.
- Static scans and AI exposure proof stay green after the change.

Suggested implementation approach

- Remove any browser-facing OpenAI env usage.
- Route all AI calls through existing server-side orchestration functions.
- Add static regressions that fail on frontend OpenAI key or direct provider client usage.

#### Service Worker Caches Too Broadly

Problem

- The service worker appears to cache too broadly and must not cache Supabase/API/authenticated/dynamic JSON responses. Caching should be restricted to safe static same-origin assets.

Risk

- Sensitive responses can be stored client-side, replayed, or served stale across users/sessions.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Service worker registration and implementation
- Workbox/Vite PWA config
- Runtime caching rules and route matchers
- Any API fetch wrappers touched by SW logic

Acceptance criteria

- Service worker caches only explicitly safe static assets.
- Supabase, auth, API, edge-function, and dynamic JSON responses are excluded from runtime cache.
- Offline/static asset behavior remains correct for safe launch surfaces.

Suggested implementation approach

- Replace permissive runtime caching with allowlisted same-origin asset rules.
- Add explicit deny rules for API/auth/Supabase/dynamic endpoints.
- Add proof covering cache headers/requests for sensitive flows.

#### Email HTML Interpolation Needs Centralized Escaping

Problem

- Edge functions that compose email HTML may interpolate user-controlled fields without consistent centralized escaping or sanitization.

Risk

- Email HTML injection can break rendering, alter outbound content, or create phishing/trust issues.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Email-sending edge functions and helper libraries
- Invite, RSVP, guest messaging, vendor, and notification email templates
- Shared string/template utilities used in email HTML composition

Acceptance criteria

- All user-controlled fields interpolated into email HTML are escaped or sanitized through one approved helper path.
- New email templates cannot bypass the helper accidentally.
- Regression tests cover representative unsafe characters/payloads.

Suggested implementation approach

- Introduce a single escaping helper for email HTML interpolation.
- Refactor edge functions/templates to use it consistently.
- Add tests around common injection characters and malformed HTML content.

#### Registry Preview Fetcher Needs SSRF Hardening

Problem

- The registry preview edge function fetches user-provided URLs and needs protocol restrictions, private IP blocking, metadata endpoint blocking, content-type checks, size limits, timeout controls, and redirect limits.

Risk

- SSRF can expose internal services, metadata endpoints, private network targets, or create denial-of-service/resource abuse paths.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Registry preview edge function
- URL validation helpers and fetch wrappers
- Registry save/preview dashboard flows
- Related proof scripts or smoke tests for registry preview

Acceptance criteria

- Only approved protocols/domains/content types are fetched.
- Private IP ranges, localhost, metadata endpoints, and excessive redirects are blocked.
- Fetches enforce timeout and response-size limits.
- Unsafe URLs fail with calm customer-safe errors.

Suggested implementation approach

- Add a hardened URL/fetch guard layer around registry preview requests.
- Resolve and reject private/internal addresses before fetch.
- Enforce response and redirect limits in the function, with regression coverage.

#### Dashboard Settings Privacy Fields May Be Read Without Being Selected

Problem

- Dashboard settings queries appear to use fields like `privacy_mode`, `hide_from_search`, `guest_access_token`, `default_language`, and `notification_prefs` without always selecting them explicitly.

Risk

- The UI can become inconsistent, write back stale/partial values, or accidentally widen query selectors later to “fix” missing data unsafely.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Dashboard settings pages/components
- Shared settings loaders and mutation helpers
- Site settings select strings and DTO mapping
- Tests for dashboard settings read/write behavior

Acceptance criteria

- Every dashboard settings consumer explicitly selects the fields it reads.
- Settings writes/readbacks stay consistent with no hidden implicit field assumptions.
- No sensitive/private-only fields are accidentally pulled into broader contexts to fix missing state.

Suggested implementation approach

- Audit settings consumers and align select strings with actual field usage.
- Create typed settings DTOs for each settings surface.
- Add regression tests for read/write round-trip consistency.

### P1 - Must Fix Before Paid Launch

#### Replace Or Isolate `xlsx` Dependency

Problem

- `npm audit` reports a production-relevant vulnerability around `xlsx`. Replace it if possible, or move parsing server-side and restrict upload/import behavior.

Risk

- Vulnerable spreadsheet parsing expands the app’s attack surface through guest import or other file ingestion flows.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Dependency manifests and lockfile
- Guest import/upload flows
- Spreadsheet parsing helpers/utilities
- Any server/import pipeline that can absorb spreadsheet parsing

Acceptance criteria

- Vulnerable `xlsx` usage is removed, replaced, or isolated behind a safer controlled path.
- Import flow still supports intended launch use cases.
- Security posture is improved and documented in backlog/proof notes.

Suggested implementation approach

- Evaluate a safer replacement first.
- If replacement is not realistic for launch, isolate parsing to a more controlled server-side path with strict file/type/size checks.
- Add focused regression proof for import behavior.

#### Fix Failed Smoke Test

Problem

- `npm run smoke:registry` failed and needs investigation and repair.

Risk

- One of the intended proof gates is already known-red, which weakens launch confidence and can hide a real user-facing registry regression.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- `scripts` and tests behind `smoke:registry`
- Registry public and dashboard flows
- Registry preview/save/render code paths

Acceptance criteria

- `npm run smoke:registry` passes reliably on current source.
- Failure root cause is documented in backlog/proof notes.

Suggested implementation approach

- Reproduce locally, isolate whether failure is test drift or real product bug, then fix the narrower root cause.
- Keep any registry smoke proof tied to launch-critical scenarios only.

#### Make Typecheck, Lint, And Tests Complete Reliably

Problem

- `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, and `npm test` timed out in review. They need to complete in a reasonable time locally and in CI.

Risk

- Slow or flaky core verification makes regressions easier to ship and discourages disciplined predeploy checks.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Package scripts and CI commands
- Vite/Vitest/Jest config as applicable
- TypeScript project references
- Overly broad test discovery or expensive setup files

Acceptance criteria

- Typecheck, lint, and test commands complete reliably within an agreed launch-friendly time budget.
- CI/local commands are clear and repeatable.

Suggested implementation approach

- Split broad commands where needed, remove redundant work, and optimize expensive test/setup paths.
- Keep a single documented “full gate” plus focused subcommands for local iteration.

#### Split Large Dashboard Files

Problem

- Large files such as `src/pages/dashboard/Guests.tsx` appear to exceed 5,000 lines and mix UI, import logic, messaging, RSVP config, audit logs, local storage, and Supabase calls.

Risk

- Bug-fixing becomes slower and riskier, testability degrades, and small changes have oversized regression blast radius.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- `src/pages/dashboard/Guests.tsx`
- Other oversized dashboard/page modules
- Related hooks/helpers already embedded in page files

Acceptance criteria

- Oversized dashboard pages are broken into coherent feature modules with clearer ownership boundaries.
- Behavior remains stable and existing proof continues to pass.

Suggested implementation approach

- Extract by feature slice first: import, RSVP config, messaging, audit/readback, shared guest UI blocks.
- Keep behavioral changes minimal during the split and add targeted regression tests around extracted seams.

#### Move Direct Supabase Calls Out Of Page Components

Problem

- Page components directly own site, guest, RSVP, registry, messaging, and settings data access.

Risk

- UI code becomes tightly coupled to query details, harder to secure, and harder to regression test.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Dashboard and public page components with inline Supabase calls
- Shared Supabase client helpers
- Feature modules for site, guest, RSVP, registry, messaging, and settings

Acceptance criteria

- Major feature surfaces use repository/service layers instead of embedding raw Supabase access in page components.
- Security-sensitive selection logic lives in shared typed data-access layers.

Suggested implementation approach

- Introduce feature-scoped repository/service modules gradually, starting with the highest-risk surfaces.
- Move select strings, DTO shaping, and mutation contracts out of TSX page files.

#### Improve Test Structure

Problem

- Unit, integration, smoke, and e2e tests are not cleanly separated, and commands are not as clear as they should be.

Risk

- Developers run the wrong proof layer, skip critical checks, or struggle to understand what is supposed to catch which class of bug.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- `package.json`
- `tests/`, `src/**/*.test.*`, and script organization
- Proof docs that reference verification commands

Acceptance criteria

- Test classes are clearly separated and named.
- There are explicit commands for unit, integration, smoke, and e2e coverage.
- Proof docs reference the right commands consistently.

Suggested implementation approach

- Reorganize scripts first, then move/rename tests where needed.
- Keep smoke/e2e naming aligned with launch-proof usage.

### P2 - Important Cleanup

#### Shrink Public Asset Footprint

Problem

- Build output appears very large because public preview GIFs/assets are copied into `dist`.

Risk

- Slower installs/builds/deploys and a worse customer load experience, especially on mobile networks.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- `public/` large media assets
- Build output and asset copy pipeline
- Template preview media strategy

Acceptance criteria

- Large demo/preview media no longer bloats the app bundle unnecessarily.
- Customer-facing load and deploy footprint improves measurably.

Suggested implementation approach

- Move heavy preview media to CDN/object storage or generate optimized thumbnails/static images for the shipped app.

#### Add File-Size/Complexity Guardrails

Problem

- There are no strong guardrails preventing new 2,000+ line page files or similarly overgrown modules.

Risk

- The codebase slides back into fragile “mega-file” patterns even after cleanup.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- ESLint config
- CI scripts/checks
- Engineering docs or contribution notes

Acceptance criteria

- CI or lint warns/fails on new file-size/complexity violations within agreed thresholds.

Suggested implementation approach

- Add lint or custom CI checks for file length and optionally complexity/hook count thresholds, with scoped exceptions where truly necessary.

#### Update Stale Audit/Release Docs

Problem

- Some docs currently appear more confident than actual local checks support.

Risk

- The team can make launch decisions from stale or overclaimed evidence.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Release notes/backlog/proof docs
- Smoke proof logs
- Deploy state references and board generation inputs

Acceptance criteria

- Audit/release/proof docs match actual passing checks and open caveats.
- No document overstates launch readiness beyond the latest evidence.

Suggested implementation approach

- Refresh docs only after the underlying checks pass, and treat proof logs as the canonical evidence source.

#### Add Security Regression Tests

Problem

- Sensitive-field leakage, RSVP lookup safety, registry preview SSRF controls, and settings privacy paths need regression tests.

Risk

- Security/privacy regressions can reappear quietly during unrelated feature work.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Existing proof/security test files
- Public site data-access tests
- RSVP, registry preview, and settings test coverage

Acceptance criteria

- Automated tests prove sensitive fields are not returned to public site, RSVP lookup, registry preview, or settings flows.

Suggested implementation approach

- Add focused regression suites around the highest-risk DTO/query/fetch boundaries rather than broad brittle UI-only tests.

#### Add Rate Limiting/Audit Logging Where Missing

Problem

- Rate limiting and audit logging appear incomplete for RSVP lookup, password attempts, registry preview fetches, AI generation, and messaging endpoints.

Risk

- Abuse, enumeration, brute force, or runaway usage can happen without enough operational visibility or throttling.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Edge functions for RSVP, site access, registry preview, AI, and messaging
- Shared logging helpers
- Any existing throttling primitives

Acceptance criteria

- Launch-critical public or semi-public endpoints have appropriate rate limiting and audit visibility.
- Sensitive repeated-failure patterns are observable.

Suggested implementation approach

- Add lightweight shared rate-limit and audit helpers, then apply them to the highest-risk endpoints first.

### P3 - Nice To Have

#### Consider React Query Or Equivalent Query/Cache Layer

Problem

- Repeated Supabase fetch logic creates inconsistent loading/error behavior across surfaces.

Risk

- UX inconsistency and duplicated client data logic continue to accumulate.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Data-heavy dashboard/public components
- Shared fetch hooks and Supabase client access

Acceptance criteria

- If adopted, query/cache behavior is more consistent and repeated fetch logic is reduced materially.

Suggested implementation approach

- Evaluate after launch-critical security/data-boundary work is stable; do not let this preempt higher-priority hardening.

#### Improve Deployment Asset Strategy

Problem

- Heavy demo/template media is shipped too directly in the app bundle/deploy output.

Risk

- Larger deploy artifacts and slower customer asset delivery persist even after basic bundle cleanup.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- Deploy/build asset pipeline
- Template/demo media storage and reference strategy

Acceptance criteria

- Deploy artifact strategy is intentional and heavy demo media is decoupled from the core app where appropriate.

Suggested implementation approach

- Follow the bundle-footprint cleanup with a more durable asset hosting/versioning plan.

#### Add Architecture Notes

Problem

- The intended boundary between frontend, Supabase client, edge functions, service-role functions, and public data access is not documented clearly enough.

Risk

- New work can reintroduce the same security and coupling mistakes because the intended architecture is implicit.

Likely files/areas to inspect

- `docs/` architecture or runbook files
- Data-access/service-layer conventions

Acceptance criteria

- Architecture notes describe which layers may access which data and where sensitive operations must live.

Suggested implementation approach

- Add a short, practical architecture note after the higher-priority boundary fixes land so it reflects the real intended system.

## Super Nice Launch Audit Backlog - 2026-05-03 7:02 PM PT

Scope: Eric asked for everything not up to the standard of a super nice launch, including bugs, UI glitches, proof gaps, and product-quality caveats. This section is broader than "active ungated launch blockers." It includes items that may be acceptable for a narrow v1, but should be fixed, proven, or explicitly accepted before a polished public push.

Evidence collected in this audit:

- Template truth matrix added: `docs/template-truth-matrix-2026-05-04.md`. Use it as the source for launch-visible template tiers until the next promotion pass.
- Focused renderer/template proof passed on 2026-05-04 2:56 PM PT: public builder-rendered pages now prefer the rich section registry, launch template surfaces are narrowed to 7 flagship and 6 secondary templates, hidden legacy templates remain addressable for saved sites, and every launch-visible template section resolves through the rich public registry.
- Flagship visual proof passed on 2026-05-04 3:06 PM PT: the seven flagship full-preview routes now start directly with the public hero, no longer show proof metadata by default, and have distinct above-the-fold hero compositions. Focused desktop/mobile Playwright proof passed 2/2 against local preview with no console errors, broken images, or horizontal overflow.
- Launch template section hardening passed on 2026-05-04 3:15 PM PT: all 13 launch-visible templates now use coherent Sayulita travel/accommodation defaults instead of stale New York placeholder logistics, and desktop/mobile browser proof passed 3/3 against local preview for first-viewport, secondary-template, stale-placeholder, unsafe-term, broken-image, console-error, and horizontal-overflow checks.

### Added In 2026-05-04 6:39 AM PT Competitive Audit Product Lane

Source sheet: `docs/wedding-website-competitive-audit-2026-05-04.md`.

Stance: These are not emergency launch blockers unless Eric explicitly promotes one. They are the "do it once and do it right" product-expansion systems pulled from the competitive audit. Do not implement them as one-off buttons or isolated screens. Each item needs product design, data model review, privacy/security review, mobile/desktop UX, migration planning where needed, and proof coverage before it is called done.

Product thesis from the audit: DayOf should not become another generic wedding website builder. The lane is a calm wedding operating system where the site, guest list, RSVP, messages, photos, seating, coordinator flow, and AI setup all know about each other.

1. Guest-specific preview and visibility confidence.
   - Priority: Highest leverage.
   - Status: Active product-expansion lane. First coherent slice shipped in the Guests drawer on 2026-05-04 8:09 AM PT; route-level owner preview banner slice shipped on 2026-05-04 10:39 AM PT; broader cross-surface personalization system remains open.
   - Competitor references: Riley & Grey, Appy Couple, Aisle, Say I do, WedSites, GettingMarried.
   - Build once/right outcome: one consistent "Preview as..." system for public visitor, guest, household, planner, coordinator, and owner views across public site, RSVP, private events, travel, guest portal, registry visibility, photo upload, and role-limited dashboard surfaces.
   - UX requirements: show who/what is being previewed, why a section/event is visible or hidden, and whether the owner is seeing a draft or published state. Provide an obvious exit back to owner mode.
   - Technical requirements: centralize visibility rules instead of duplicating conditionals per page; cover household, event invite, plus-one, child, language, planner/coordinator role, and public/password states.
   - Proof bar: owner can pick a guest/household/role and see matching site, RSVP, event, photo, and coordinator views on desktop and mobile; automated tests prove at least one private event is hidden from the wrong guest and visible to the right guest.
   - Batch 1 shipped: central `src/lib/guestVisibilityPreview.ts` helper now summarizes guest-visible events, hidden events, household state, safe preview links, and owner warnings without rendering raw invite tokens in labels/details. The Guests itinerary drawer now shows a Visibility preview panel with RSVP preview, public-site context preview, copy-preview-link action, visible/hidden event chips, household summary, and launch-safety warnings.
   - Batch 1 proof: `npm run test -- --run src/lib/guestVisibilityPreview.test.ts` passed 4/4, `npm run proof:v1:guests-rsvp-ops` passed 3/3, `npm run typecheck -- --pretty false` passed, `npm run lint -- --quiet` passed, `git diff --check` passed, `npm run build` passed, and `npm run proof:v1:board:md` regenerated the proof board at 2026-05-04 8:10 AM PT. No deploy was run.
   - Batch 2 shipped: central `src/lib/ownerPreviewMode.ts` now detects guest, role, and public owner-preview params, strips only preview params for the leave-preview href, and avoids rendering guest ids, private access values, provider details, or raw diagnostics. `src/components/site/OwnerPreviewBanner.tsx` renders a visible preview banner on RSVP, Event Hub, and public-site render paths. Guest preview links now include explicit `previewSurface` context while keeping private invite values out of display copy.
   - Batch 2 proof: `npm run test -- --run src/lib/ownerPreviewMode.test.ts src/lib/guestVisibilityPreview.test.ts` passed 8/8 after the known Vite temp-file permission rerun, `npm run typecheck -- --pretty false` passed, `npm run lint -- --quiet` passed, `git diff --check` passed, `npm run build` passed after the known Vite temp-file permission rerun, `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:4185 npx playwright test --workers=1 tests/e2e/mobile-core-smoke.spec.ts --grep "guest-facing mobile routes"` passed 1/1 after the Playwright test-results permission rerun, `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:4185 npx playwright test --workers=1 tests/e2e/public-site-quality.spec.ts` passed 4/4, and `npm run proof:v1:board:md` regenerated the proof board at 2026-05-04 10:40 AM PT. No deploy was run.
   - Still open: true route-level public-site personalization beyond the owner-preview banner, planner/coordinator role preview wiring from dashboard controls, photo/registry/travel visibility preview, mobile browser click proof for the Guests drawer panel itself, and cross-surface automated tests that prove private event visibility is hidden/visible in every relevant guest-facing view.

2. Unified QR guest hub.
   - Priority: Highest leverage.
   - Status: Active product-expansion lane. First coherent shared-action/link slice shipped on 2026-05-04 8:35 AM PT; public printable QR asset pack shipped on 2026-05-04 10:47 AM PT; guest-specific QR privacy modes, revocation/rotation, and live production proof remain open.
   - Competitor references: Wedibox, GUESTPIX, Honeyfund, Say I do, Invitatis, Online-RSVP.
   - Build once/right outcome: one wedding QR hub that routes guests to the right actions: RSVP, public site, schedule, directions, registry, photo upload, guestbook/vault, travel, and day-of updates.
   - UX requirements: simple guest landing page, no app requirement, readable on event signage, strong empty/error states, and clear privacy for guest-specific links vs public QR.
   - Technical requirements: stable hub URL model, QR generation, per-surface deep links, printable/exportable assets, link revocation/rotation where guest-specific tokens are involved.
   - Proof bar: generated QR assets open correctly on mobile, support public and guest-specific modes, avoid leaking invite tokens in public contexts, and are printable/exportable.
   - Batch 1 shipped: central `src/lib/guestHubActions.ts` now builds the guest hub action set from one slug/settings contract for RSVP, schedule, travel, registry, photo upload, guestbook, and recap. `/event/:siteRef` now uses that shared model, expands beyond memories-only actions, and honors the existing hub controls. The owner Memories page now shows the current action set next to the QR panel and reuses the same action model for QR confidence copy.
   - Batch 1 proof: `npm run test -- --run src/lib/guestHubActions.test.ts src/pages/EventHub.test.tsx` passed 9/9, `npm run typecheck -- --pretty false` passed, `npm run lint -- --quiet` passed, `git diff --check` passed, `npm run build` passed, and `npm run proof:v1:board:md` regenerated the proof board at 2026-05-04 8:35 AM PT. No deploy was run.
   - Batch 2 shipped: central `src/lib/guestHubQrAssets.ts` now builds a public guest-hub print pack for welcome sign, table card, invitation insert, and photo prompt, renders escaped print-ready HTML with QR images, and blocks token-like/private URL params from printable public assets. The Memories dashboard now offers "Save print cards" from the One QR guest hub panel.
   - Batch 2 proof: `npm run test -- --run src/lib/guestHubQrAssets.test.ts src/lib/guestHubActions.test.ts` passed 7/7 after the known Vite temp-file permission rerun. The first focused proof caught `invite_token`/camel-case token-like params not being blocked; the detector was tightened and rerun green. `npm run typecheck -- --pretty false` passed, `npm run lint -- --quiet` passed, `git diff --check` passed, `npm run build` passed after the known Vite temp-file permission rerun, `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:4186 npx playwright test --workers=1 tests/e2e/mobile-core-smoke.spec.ts --grep "authenticated dashboard core routes"` passed 1/1 with the Memories print-card action included, and `npm run proof:v1:board:md` regenerated the proof board at 2026-05-04 10:48 AM PT. No deploy was run.
   - Still open: guest-specific QR mode that never prints private invite tokens accidentally, QR revocation/rotation model, vault/day-of update deep links, real downloaded print-pack visual inspection/nonblank QR proof, live production mobile proof for generated QR links, and owner proof that public and guest-specific hub modes route to the right actions without leaking private access.

3. Status-based messaging and invitation tracking.
   - Priority: Highest leverage.
   - Status: Active product-expansion lane. First operational audience-segment slice shipped on 2026-05-04 8:42 AM PT; missing address and meal segment slice shipped on 2026-05-04 10:53 AM PT; delivery/open/view tracking normalization and live proof remain open.
   - Competitor references: Greenvelope, Paperless Post, The Knot, Joy, WedSites.
   - Build once/right outcome: messaging shifts from a blank composer to smart operational segments: not sent, sent, opened, viewed, RSVP pending, missing meal, missing address, bounced, delivery failed, needs review, and safe to remind.
   - UX requirements: owner chooses a segment, previews recipients, reviews editable copy, sees delivery/open/responded state, and can retry or exclude problem recipients without exposing provider internals.
   - Technical requirements: normalize delivery/open/view/RSVP state across email/SMS/future channels, preserve SMS/Telnyx deferred gates, keep provider details internal, and protect unsubscribe/contact preferences.
   - Proof bar: tests and live proof show segment counts, safe copy, delivery-failure sanitization, recipient preview, and no provider/key/raw error leakage.
   - Batch 1 shipped: central `src/lib/messageAudienceSegments.ts` now defines composer audience segments and filtering for all guests, attending, RSVP pending, declined, invite not sent, invited/no reply, reminder sent/no reply, and itinerary-event audiences. The Messages composer loads `invitation_sent_at` and `reminder_last_sent_at`, uses the shared filter for recipient counts, and shows a plain-language detail for the selected audience before sending.

- Added guest dashboard `Preview` action that opens the exact tokenized RSVP flow for a selected guest.
- Expanded vendor profile generation from one shell into selectable persisted template variants: editorial, portfolio, and minimal.
- Rendered public vendor pages with variant-specific layout treatment using `source_payload.template_id`, avoiding schema churn.
- Expanded the planner starter suite beyond tasks, budget, and vendors into timeline seeds, RSVP question seeds, travel FAQ seeds, photo bucket seeds, and guest import suggestions.
- Added planner preview chips for the expanded starter suite so couples can see what will be added before applying.
- Added sanitized client-side Edge Function failure telemetry through the Supabase fetch hook and existing internal error logger.
- Started the calm wedding UI rework using the supplied mock direction: warmer off-white palette, softer sage accent, subtler card treatment, and zero negative letter spacing.
- Reworked the dashboard shell language and navigation from admin/ops language into calmer wedding language: `dayof`, pinned navigation, Schedule, Memories, Planner, Day-of view, Vaults, and Activity.
- Reworked the dashboard overview hero and next-step surface into a calmer wedding home with softer labels, fewer business-style phrases, and gentler action copy.
- Gated internal proof cards behind `?proof=1` so normal couples do not see launch-proof/debug-style panels in the overview.
- Refreshed login/signup/onboarding entry branding from `WeddingSite` into `dayof` and softened account/setup copy.
- Expanded launch wording guard coverage to block business-style launch-path phrases such as `command center`, `engagement dashboard`, `launch score`, and `site visibility` from critical customer surfaces.
- Removed more customer-facing operations/provider language from guests, registry, messages, photos, planner links, RSVP board, vault contribution, planner access descriptions, and analytics labels.
- Replaced old browser/app metadata (`Dayof Wedding SaaS Frontend`, Bolt OG image, dark theme color) with `dayof`, warm theme color, and wedding-native descriptions.
- Expanded launch wording guard coverage to include app metadata, manifest, planning links, RSVP board, vault contribution, planner access copy, analytics labels, and provider-detail phrases.
- Refreshed mobile smoke coverage so it tracks the current calmer page names: `Wedding home` and `Collect guest photos around the moments you care about`.
- Added an image-led dashboard hero rail using the local wedding preview image so the overview feels warmer and less like a text-only admin page.

- Builder/public hero sections now keep existing hero media visible when opacity is stored as a fraction, so default/template heroes do not degrade into gray placeholders.
- Builder/public identity now repairs stale saved `The couple` placeholders from canonical site names before rendering.
- Overview default now keeps readiness/proof/archive/moderation-style detail behind `Show more detail`.
- Messages default now keeps sending diagnostics, text credit ledger, and reach details behind `Show sending details`.
- Guest hub default now reads like a guest-facing wedding product, not a QR setup page, and hides raw URLs from the main visual surface.
- Public registry cards now have polished missing-image fallbacks so retailer image blocks do not make the public site look unfinished.
- Additional customer-visible workspace/operator/ops/readiness phrasing has been softened across the highest-traffic owner and guest surfaces.
- Overview setup progress now visually matches the completed state instead of showing a short partial fill next to `6/6 complete`.
- Post-wedding name-change planning now uses calmer couple-facing labels and hides diagnostic movement detail behind review mode instead of showing internal execution terminology by default.
- Quick-start and AI onboarding no longer leave production-facing runtime/provider chatter in the browser console by default.
- Vendor template browsing now uses customer-facing source labels like `Website ready`, `Social profile`, `Image starter`, and `Needs images`, with local browser smoke proof refreshed after the copy change.
- Vendor page creation copy now frames the flow as shaping a polished vendor page instead of exposing internal source/context mapping.
- Guided setup now points people toward `wedding home`, `site editor`, `Schedule`, `Travel`, and `Guests` instead of repeatedly saying dashboard/builder.
- Memories now uses album/moment language across the main owner surface, slideshow, schedule suggestions, upload links, and QR sharing.
- Registry item cards now use calmer owner labels such as `Product image`, `Backup image`, `Needs image`, `Needs repair`, and `Worth checking` instead of fallback/import jargon.
- Settings visibility, collaborator invite success, Day-of view, and Messages templates had another pass to remove launch/ops/dashboard phrasing from visible copy.
- Public privacy copy now says service updates instead of operational updates.
- Day-of coordinator mode now uses suggested-action and summary language instead of command/board-target/alert-lane language, while preserving the existing permission-aware controls and coordinator tests.
- Remaining negative letter-spacing classes were removed from customer-facing React surfaces so text rendering is calmer and more predictable across desktop and mobile.
- Remaining uppercase and letter-spacing utility classes were removed from dashboard, dashboard component, and builder control surfaces so compact labels feel less like a B2B console.
- RSVP, Registry, Vaults, Memories, Messages, Seating, and Overview had another route-level copy pass to replace lifecycle, fallback, prospect, cap-check, and work-queue language with calmer wedding planning language.
- Post-wedding name-change planning had another focused copy pass so status chips and step summaries read as human next steps rather than workflow/execution terminology.
- Public fallback, payment-required, section manifest, and name-change overview copy now use site-editor/saved-status language instead of builder-owned/manual-bucket/dashboard phrasing.
- Public vault, vendor profile, missing-site, and guided setup guest-import dead-end copy now avoids not-found/manual-work language on critical customer paths.
- Dev-speed tooling is now in place: `$dayof-qa` skill validation, consolidated local gate/proof commands, changed-route smoke suggestions, screenshot capture, and generated Supabase TypeScript types.
- Older wedding-status onboarding, seating empty-state, and planner starter-suite copy now avoid not-found/manual/dashboard/key language and are covered by launch wording guard.
- Guests assisted-RSVP and exception-review controls now use assisted/personal language instead of manual-decision/manual-RSVP labels, with route-level guard coverage.
- Guest import mapping and event-name review copy now uses invitation/schedule matching language instead of RSVP-link-ID and not-found phrasing.
- First-run status/setup surfaces now say wedding home/design instead of dashboard/template-ID/template-selection language, with guard coverage.
- Guest photo upload visual proof caught and fixed low-contrast hero text; fresh desktop/mobile screenshots are saved under `docs/proof-screenshots/2026-05-02/dayof-ui-1777731851762`.
- Guest memory surfaces now sanitize public guest hub and recap opt-in errors, convert recap bucket names into album labels, and remove remaining compact all-caps/letter-spaced labels from the touched guest hub, recap, and guestbook surfaces.
- Quick-start setup, guest photo upload, template detail, and trust pages now have another public-label softening pass so key labels feel less like a console or spec sheet.
- Vendor page creation and public vendor profiles now use calmer design/page labels and no longer show all-caps/letter-spaced spec styling on their main public surfaces.
- Vendor template browser and sample preview cards now use calmer labels across selected design, vendor browser, review checklist, inquiry inbox, and source-quality chips, with gated browser proof green.
- Latest no-deploy canonical V1 proof passed 3/3 after the public/vendor hardening: build integrity, 35/35 public live-smoke routes, and Supabase site lookup smoke.
- Memories now connects guest albums to Vaults with a softer keepsake panel instead of a heavy dark promo band.
- Vaults now opens with the shared calm page hero, private-keepsake framing, simple stats, and an `Add vault` action.
- Messages quick starts now use calmer wedding-update labels and photo request copy instead of launchpad/product funnel wording.
- Planner starter-suite and page-mode copy now reads like helpful wedding planning, not operating plans, access machinery, or vendor lead tracking.
- The builder variant gallery now uses site-editor/layout-gallery language and warmer design review labels while preserving advanced quality filters.
- Guests, Messages, and Day-of view now use `text` language for owner-facing texting surfaces while keeping live provider sending deferred.
- Builder media uploads, builder publish feedback, public vendor profiles, setup save recovery, and Vault contribution uploads now use customer-safe retry/recovery copy instead of leaking storage, provider, bucket, function, or database details.
- Checkout/payment, quick-start AI onboarding recovery, scheduled-message actions, RSVP settings, registry refresh settings, Vault owner actions, song requests, address collection, public photo upload, recap loading, and vendor page creation now use safe retry copy instead of exposing provider/function/database/network/policy errors.
- Registry gift import guidance, name-change document summaries, collaborator invite QA details, and builder section setting labels had another calm-language pass so couples see gift, document, invite, and site-design language instead of manual cleanup, metadata, diagnostics, or form-builder labels.
- Messaging history now frames repeat sends as follow-up review and `Send again` instead of retry queues, while preserving delivery review safeguards and locked provider behavior.
- Public Guests and Registry feature copy no longer says manual override/manual cleanup for duplicate handling or gift import details.
- Public Home, Guests, Registry, and Seating feature pages had another by-hand/manual-wording cleanup after screenshot review, and refreshed desktop/mobile screenshots were saved under `docs/proof-screenshots/2026-05-02/dayof-ui-1777744119585`.
- Latest proof refresh passed locally with focused registry/name-change/builder-label/message/photo tests, `npm run dayof:gate`, local browser launch/mobile smoke, live prereqs, and anon-limited data integrity. No deploy was run.
- Remaining UI work is no longer an active rework lane. UI changes should now come only from the sitewide bug-testing matrix: broken interactions, mobile overflow, text clipping, inaccessible states, visual regressions, trust/security wording leaks, or accepted launch-blocking usability bugs.

Mock signals reviewed:

- `app/styles/theme.css`: off-white background, soft cards, muted gray text, sage accent, subtle borders.
- `app/components/HeroSection.tsx`: calm greeting, wedding countdown, single primary next step, image-led warmth.
- `app/components/CoreCards.tsx`: compact wedding modules with gentle status text instead of dense business metrics.
- `app/components/Sidebar.tsx`: simple dayof navigation, pinned core items, More expansion, low visual noise.

Baseline goals now met:

- Reduce the current dashboard and tool pages feeling like a business command center.
- Remove unnecessary framed panels, labels, badges, hard outlines, redundant headings, and over-explained UI text.
- Replace SaaS/operations language with calm wedding language that sounds like a couple is planning a meaningful day, not managing enterprise software.
- Keep structure and power, but make the default experience feel quieter, warmer, and easier to trust.
- Preserve advanced controls, but move them behind clear secondary affordances instead of making them the default surface.

Completed implementation scope:

- Rework the main dashboard into a calm wedding home: greeting, countdown, current next step, wedding image, and a few clear modules.
- Rework navigation toward pinned core items plus a More area so the app does not feel overloaded at first glance.
- Apply a calmer shared visual system across overview, builder, guests, photos, planning, registry, messages, settings, vendor profiles, and guest-facing flows.
- Replace repeated card containers with breathable grouped layouts, soft dividers, inline rows, and full-width sections where appropriate.
- Standardize card density: fewer labels, shorter headings, human status copy, and only show metrics when they help the couple act.
- Tune colors toward the mock direction: warm off-white backgrounds, white cards, subtle borders, muted gray copy, sage or soft green accent.
- Make mobile calmer: thumb-friendly actions, fewer stacked panels, less label overwhelm, no clipped text, no overlapping controls.
- Bring builder sections/templates closer to the same standard so public site editing feels emotional, visual, and custom rather than form-heavy.
- Refresh stock/image usage where the page needs emotional context, especially dashboard, builder, photos, registry, vendor profiles, and public guest paths.

Ongoing regression bar:

- Product should feel premium, calm, and wedding-native, not like a repurposed B2B admin dashboard.
- Important actions should remain obvious without adding instructional copy everywhere.
- Advanced customization should exist, but the default path should give 90% of couples only the choices they need.
- Every touched route should get desktop and mobile screenshots or browser proof.
- No regressions to payment bypass, public RSVP links, guest upload, vault, registry, builder publishing, or planner flows.

Done status:

- The mock direction is visibly reflected in the real app shell and core dashboards.
- The main product pages have fewer cluttered containers and less business-style labeling.
- Desktop and mobile visual QA has been repeatedly exercised through local browser proof during the UI pass.
- Remaining UI action is regression/bug testing only, not foundational launch-blocking rework.

## Deferred - External Setup Or Secure Proof Environment

- Native Instagram/TikTok story posting through mobile share sheet or native app integration.
- Secure service-role data integrity proof for private storage buckets and full cross-table orphan checks. Current anon-limited data integrity proof is green.
- Direct local proof of server-side AI secret values. Secret name is configured server-side; value should not be exposed locally.
- Final OpenAI key rotation is external security work because the previous key was shared in chat.
- Face clustering or identity recognition unless privacy posture and explicit user controls are defined.
- Real-time live event wall/display mode.
- Voice guestbook with transcription.
- Advanced to-scale floorplan editor with object library, versioning, PDF/image export, and vendor share roles.
- Full timeline dependency graph beyond the current basic cascade shifting.
- White-label vendor/coordinator portals.

## Later - Product Expansion

- Instagram-style guest event memory feed:
  - Mobile-first vertical feed of approved event photos/videos.
  - Guests can scroll, favorite, and optionally leave a lightweight caption or memory.
  - Couple sees most-loved photos in the dashboard.
  - Feed can seed Best of Wedding, recap, and slideshow drafts.
  - Keep this as an event memory feed, not a full social network with profiles, comments, notifications, or social graph.
